# HMM-1 — Utils - normalize, log-normalize and fill covariances

> Forgejo issue #1 · closed · opened 2026-10-07 10:51:38 UTC by `claude` · closed 2026-10-07 12:51:12 UTC  
> Labels: `state/merged` · Milestone: J1

**Spec (authoritative):** [`.factory/tickets/HMM-1.yml`](https://github.com/Simon-Bu/hmmlearn-net/blob/main/.factory/tickets/HMM-1.yml)

**Objective.** Port hmmlearn/utils.py to C#: the static class HmmLearn.Utils with Normalize, LogNormalize and FillCovars, which every later
module uses. This is the calibration ticket of the project: the smallest item.
Reference: reference/hmmlearn-0.3.3/src/hmmlearn/utils.py (71 lines), its Python tests
reference/hmmlearn-0.3.3/src/hmmlearn/tests/test_utils.py, and the numbers of oracle/golden/utils.json (its structure is
described in oracle/README.md; do not open the JSON file whole, it is large).

**Criteria**

- **C1** — Utils.Normalize divides in place by the sum, for a vector and for a matrix with axis null, 0 and 1; a zero sum leaves the zeros;
an invalid axis throws ArgumentOutOfRangeException. Every case of normalize_cases of oracle/golden/utils.json is reproduced.
 · `dotnet test tests/HmmLearn.Tests --filter "FullyQualifiedName~HmmLearn.Tests.Acceptance.Hmm1.NormalizeTests"`

- **C2** — Utils.LogNormalize subtracts the log-sum-exp in place, for a vector and for a matrix with axis null, 0 and 1, including the
special cases of the interface (length-1 axis gives zeros, -infinity entries, a row of -infinity gives NaN). Every case of
log_normalize_cases of oracle/golden/utils.json is reproduced.
 · `dotnet test tests/HmmLearn.Tests --filter "FullyQualifiedName~HmmLearn.Tests.Acceptance.Hmm1.LogNormalizeTests"`

- **C3** — Utils.FillCovars expands the compact covariances of the four covariance types to (nc, nf, nf) matrices and rejects a shape
that does not match with ArgumentException. Every case of fill_covars_cases of oracle/golden/utils.json is reproduced.
 · `dotnet test tests/HmmLearn.Tests --filter "FullyQualifiedName~HmmLearn.Tests.Acceptance.Hmm1.FillCovarsTests"`

- **C4** — The code stays readable: short methods, no dead code, the XML summaries match the behaviour. · judged by the reviewer

**Roles.** tests: `qwen-q4-long` · coder: `qwen-pi` · reviewer: `qwen-q4-long` · 3 rounds at most

**Writable scope.** `src/HmmLearn/Utils.cs`

**Waiver: reviewer from the same family as the coder.** POC local-only decided by Simon

_Published by the pilot. The ticket's state is carried by the `state/…` labels, updated by the orchestrator._

      "context": "The axis-1 expectation for row {2.0, 2.0} is wrong. That row sums to 4.0, so dividing in place by the row sum gives {0.5, 0.5}, but the test expects {1.0, 1.0}. A row-normalized row must sum to 1 (or stay all-zero for a zero row); {1, 1} sums to 2. The golden case matrix-rows confirms division by the sum (row {0.3044522, 0.9781174, 0.1273876} -> {0.2159301, 0.6937213, 0.0903485}), and the implementation reproduces it exactly; the expectation {1, 1} would only be produced by max-normalization, which contradicts the interface (divide by the sum).",
      "origin": {
        "role": "coder",
        "agent": "qwen-pi",
        "round": 1,
        "id": "Q1"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "Q5",
      "blocking": false,
      "question": "The coder disputes the acceptance test \"HmmLearn.Tests.Acceptance.Hmm1.LogNormalizeTests.LogNormalize_vector_is_stable_for_extreme_values\". Should it be fixed?",
      "context": "The large-values expectation is wrong. For {1000, 1000} the log-sum-exp is 1000 + ln(2), so the in-place result is {-ln(2), -ln(2)} ~= {-0.693147, -0.693147}, but the test expects {1000 - ln(2), 1000 - ln(2)} ~= {999.31, 999.31}. Log-normalizing a constant vector {c, c} always yields {-ln(2), -ln(2)} for any c, which is exactly what the small-values half of this same test expects for {-1000, -1000}; the two halves are mutually inconsistent. The expected values also break the documented invariant sum(exp(a)) == 1: exp(1000-ln2) + exp(1000-ln2) = 2*exp(1000), not 1.",
      "origin": {
        "role": "coder",
        "agent": "qwen-pi",
        "round": 1,
        "id": "Q2"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "Q6",
      "blocking": false,
      "question": "The coder disputes the acceptance test \"HmmLearn.Tests.Acceptance.Hmm1.LogNormalizeTests.LogNormalize_matrix_minus_infinity_entries_follow_the_documented_rules\". Should it be fixed?",
      "context": "The first assertion's expectation for row {-Infinity, 0} is wrong. LSE({-inf, 0}) = ln(e^-inf + e^0) = ln(1) = 0, so the result is {-inf, 0}, but the test expects {-inf, -rowLse} with rowLse = LSE({0, -1}) ~= 0.3132617. That expectation only fits the golden case rows-with-minus-infinity, whose row 0 is {-inf, 0, -1} (3 columns) -> {-inf, -rowLse, -1-rowLse}; the hand-checked 2x2 test appears to have been derived from that golden case while keeping the 3-column expectation for a 2-column row. The implementation reproduces the golden case exactly (the golden-driven test passes).",
      "origin": {
        "role": "coder",
        "agent": "qwen-pi",
        "round": 1,
        "id": "Q3"
      }
    }

### `orchestrateur` · 2026-10-07 12:28:59 UTC

<!-- anymac:journal -->
**Factory journal** — Pilot decision on **Q1**: No. The length-1 rule belongs to the matrix overload with an explicit axis only; the vector overload is a pure log-sum-exp (a lone -infinity gives NaN). The tests are right.

    "The length-1-axis rule ('If the chosen axis has length 1, every element becomes 0, even -infinity') applies to matrix axes only; the axis-less vector overload is pure stable log-sum-exp, so a lone -infinity vector yields NaN (and a lone finite value yields 0, as the golden single-column/single-row matrix cases already cover).",
    "'If the maximum is infinite it is the log-sum-exp itself' applies to +infinity as well as -infinity: for input containing +inf the result is value - inf, i.e. -inf for finite values and NaN for the +inf entries themselves.",
    "'A shape that does not match the covariance type, nComponents or nFeatures throws ArgumentException' is read as exactly ArgumentException (Assert.Throws<ArgumentException>, subclass not accepted), mirroring how the bad-axis rule is tested with exactly ArgumentOutOfRangeException.",
    "Per the 'static, pure methods, no global state' constraint, FillCovars does not modify the input array: the returned (nc,nf,nf) tensor is a new array and the compact input is byte-for-byte unchanged.",
    "In the golden file, axis null (JSON) maps to C# null (the default whole/implicit axis); the golden loops additionally assert that every vector case really has axis null."
  ],

  "questions": [
    {
      "id": "Q1",
      "question": "Does the length-1 -> zeros rule of LogNormalize also apply to the axis-less vector overload? The tests assume no (pure log-sum-exp: LogNormalize(new double[] { -5 }) == { 0 }, but LogNormalize(new double[] { double.NegativeInfinity }) == { NaN }). If the port applies the rule to vectors as well, LogNormalize_vector_of_one_element_and_of_negative_infinity will fail on its -infinity half.",
      "blocking": false,
      "origin": {
        "role": "tests",
        "agent": "qwen-q4-long",
        "round": 1,
        "id": "Q1"
      },
      "answer": "No. The length-1 rule belongs to the matrix overload with an explicit axis only; the vector overload is a pure log-sum-exp (a lone -infinity gives NaN). The tests are right."
    },
    {
      "id": "Q2",
      "question": "For FillCovars shape mismatch, is exactly ArgumentException required, or is any subclass acceptable? The tests assert the exact type with Assert.Throws<ArgumentException>; if a subclass (e.g. a derived argument exception) is intended, the 11 InlineData cases of Fill_covars_rejects_a_shape_that_does_not_match_the_type would need Assert.ThrowsAny.",
      "blocking": false,
      "origin": {
        "role": "tests",
        "agent": "qwen-q4-long",
        "round": 1,
        "id": "Q2"
      },
      "answer": "Exactly ArgumentException, as the interface says; a derived exception (for example ArgumentOutOfRangeException) is not accepted for a shape mismatch. The tests are right."
    },

      "question": "The coder disputes the acceptance test \"HmmLearn.Tests.Acceptance.Hmm1.LogNormalizeTests.LogNormalize_vector_is_stable_for_extreme_values\". Should it be fixed?",
      "context": "The large-values expectation is wrong. For {1000, 1000} the log-sum-exp is 1000 + ln(2), so the in-place result is {-ln(2), -ln(2)} ~= {-0.693147, -0.693147}, but the test expects {1000 - ln(2), 1000 - ln(2)} ~= {999.31, 999.31}. Log-normalizing a constant vector {c, c} always yields {-ln(2), -ln(2)} for any c, which is exactly what the small-values half of this same test expects for {-1000, -1000}; the two halves are mutually inconsistent. The expected values also break the documented invariant sum(exp(a)) == 1: exp(1000-ln2) + exp(1000-ln2) = 2*exp(1000), not 1.\nAddition (coder, qwen-pi, round 2): Please fix the expectation in tests/acceptance/HMM-1/LogNormalizeTests.cs:60: for {1000.0, 1000.0} expect {-Math.Log(2.0), -Math.Log(2.0)} instead of {1000.0 - Math.Log(2.0), 1000.0 - Math.Log(2.0)} (LSE({1000, 1000}) = 1000 + ln 2).       "question": "The coder disputes the acceptance test \"HmmLearn.Tests.Acceptance.Hmm1.LogNormalizeTests.LogNormalize_matrix_minus_infinity_entries_follow_the_documented_rules\". Should it be fixed?",
      "context": "The first assertion's expectation for row {-Infinity, 0} is wrong. LSE({-inf, 0}) = ln(e^-inf + e^0) = ln(1) = 0, so the result is {-inf, 0}, but the test expects {-inf, -rowLse} with rowLse = LSE({0, -1}) ~= 0.3132617. That expectation only fits the golden case rows-with-minus-infinity, whose row 0 is {-inf, 0, -1} (3 columns) -> {-inf, -rowLse, -1-rowLse}; the hand-checked 2x2 test appears to have been derived from that golden case while keeping the 3-column expectation for a 2-column row. The implementation reproduces the golden case exactly (the golden-driven test passes).\nAddition (coder, qwen-pi, round 2): Please fix the expectation in tests/acceptance/HMM-1/LogNormalizeTests.cs:134: for the row {-Infinity, 0.0} expect {double.NegativeInfinity, 0.0} instead of {double.NegativeInfinity, -rowLse} (LSE({-inf, 0}) = ln(1) = 0). With all three one-line fixes (Q4, Q5, Q6), the full suite passes 41/41. — The test applies the LSE of row {0, -1} to the row {-inf, 0}, apparently derived from the golden 3-column row [-inf, 0, -1] by dropping the -1 while keeping its LSE; the golden cases rows-with-minus-infinity and row-of-minus-infinity-gives-nan are reproduced exactly by the implementation. Still unfixed in the worktree, still fails (verify-r1.log, LogNormalizeTests.cs:134). — Recommendation: Change line 134 to: Close.Equal(new double[,] { { double.NegativeInfinity, 0.0 }, { -rowLse, -1.0 - rowLse } }, rows, \"row with -infinity\");\nAddition (coder, qwen-pi, round 2): tests/acceptance/HMM-1/LogNormalizeTests.cs:134: for the row {-Infinity, 0.0} the expectation should be {double.NegativeInfinity, 0.0}, not {double.NegativeInfinity, -rowLse}. LSE({-inf, 0}) = max(-inf, 0) + ln(e^-inf + e^0) = 0 + ln(1) = 0, so the row becomes {-inf, 0}. The test uses rowLse = ln(1 + e^-1) = 0.31326168751822286, which is the LSE of the other row {0, -1} (correct for that row, and equal to the golden case row-of-minus-infinity-gives-nan); the 2-column row {-inf, 0} appears derived from the golden rows-with-minus-infinity row [-inf, 0, -1] by dropping the -1 while keeping its 3-column LSE. No log-sum-exp satisfies LSE({-inf, 0}) = ln(1 + e^-1) because e^-inf + e^0 = 1, not 1 + e^-1. The second block of the test (column of -infinity -> NaN) is mathematically correct. One-line fix: expect {double.NegativeInfinity, 0.0} for the first row.\nAddition (coder, qwen-pi, round 3): Please fix the expectation in tests/acceptance/HMM-1/LogNormalizeTests.cs:134: for the row {-Infinity, 0.0} expect {double.NegativeInfinity, 0.0} instead of {double.NegativeInfinity, -rowLse} (LSE({-inf, 0}) = ln(1) = 0). With all three one-line fixes (Q4, Q5, Q6), the full suite passes 41/41. — The test applies the LSE of row {0, -1} to the row {-inf, 0}, apparently derived from the golden 3-column row [-inf, 0, -1] by dropping the -1 while keeping its LSE; the golden cases rows-with-minus-infinity and row-of-minus-infinity-gives-nan are reproduced exactly by the implementation. Re-verified in round 3: in-process run of all 41 tests gives 38 pass / 3 fail, this test among the three, with the same message as verify-r2.log (row with -infinity[0, 1]: expected -0.31326168751822286, got 0). This is the last code round (3/3): the verification cannot pass until the test is fixed, hence blocking. — Recommendation: Change line 134 to: Close.Equal(new double[,] { { double.NegativeInfinity, 0.0 }, { -rowLse, -1.0 - rowLse } }, rows, \"row with -infinity\");",

      "context": "The large-values expectation is wrong. Fixed by the pilot in commit 6dee331 of the ticket branch (LogNormalizeTests.cs, the first assertion of the minus-infinity test). Keep the implementation as it is: it was verified against all 41 tests in Docker.

  "questions": [
    {
      "id": "Q1",
      "question": "Does the length-1 -> zeros rule of LogNormalize also apply to the axis-less vector overload? The tests assume no (pure log-sum-exp: LogNormalize(new double[] { -5 }) == { 0 }, but LogNormalize(new double[] { double.NegativeInfinity }) == { NaN }). If the port applies the rule to vectors as well, LogNormalize_vector_of_one_element_and_of_negative_infinity will fail on its -infinity half.",
      "blocking": false,
      "origin": {
        "role": "tests",
        "agent": "qwen-q4-long",
        "round": 1,
        "id": "Q1"
      },
      "answer": "No. The length-1 rule belongs to the matrix overload with an explicit axis only; the vector overload is a pure log-sum-exp (a lone -infinity gives NaN). The tests are right."
    },
    {
      "id": "Q2",
      "question": "For FillCovars shape mismatch, is exactly ArgumentException required, or is any subclass acceptable? The tests assert the exact type with Assert.Throws<ArgumentException>; if a subclass (e.g. a derived argument exception) is intended, the 11 InlineData cases of Fill_covars_rejects_a_shape_that_does_not_match_the_type would need Assert.ThrowsAny.",
      "blocking": false,
      "origin": {
        "role": "tests",
        "agent": "qwen-q4-long",
        "round": 1,
        "id": "Q2"
      },
      "answer": "Exactly ArgumentException, as the interface says; a derived exception (for example ArgumentOutOfRangeException) is not accepted for a shape mismatch. The tests are right."
    },

      "context": "The large-values expectation is wrong.       "context": "The first assertion's expectation for row {-Infinity, 0} is wrong. That expectation only fits the golden case rows-with-minus-infinity, whose row 0 is {-inf, 0, -1} (3 columns) -> {-inf, -rowLse, -1-rowLse}; the hand-checked 2x2 test appears to have been derived from that golden case while keeping the 3-column expectation for a 2-column row. With all three one-line fixes (Q4, Q5, Q6), the full suite passes 41/41. — The test applies the LSE of row {0, -1} to the row {-inf, 0}, apparently derived from the golden 3-column row [-inf, 0, -1] by dropping the -1 while keeping its LSE; the golden cases rows-with-minus-infinity and row-of-minus-infinity-gives-nan are reproduced exactly by the implementation. LSE({-inf, 0}) = max(-inf, 0) + ln(e^-inf + e^0) = 0 + ln(1) = 0, so the row becomes {-inf, 0}. The test uses rowLse = ln(1 + e^-1) = 0.31326168751822286, which is the LSE of the other row {0, -1} (correct for that row, and equal to the golden case row-of-minus-infinity-gives-nan); the 2-column row {-inf, 0} appears derived from the golden rows-with-minus-infinity row [-inf, 0, -1] by dropping the -1 while keeping its 3-column LSE. No log-sum-exp satisfies LSE({-inf, 0}) = ln(1 + e^-1) because e^-inf + e^0 = 1, not 1 + e^-1. The second block of the test (column of -infinity -> NaN) is mathematically correct.
